+def finite_covariance_proxy_check(quick: bool) -> Check:
+    """Stress the proxy cross-entropy asymptotic on anisotropic atomic laws."""
+
+    rows = []
+    passed = True
+    sample_power = 18
+    times = (4.0, 7.0, 12.0) if quick else (4.0, 7.0, 12.0, 20.0)
+
+    for d in (4, 7):
+        sobol = qmc.Sobol(d=d + 1, scramble=True, seed=1729 + d)
+        uniform = sobol.random_base2(sample_power)
+        normal = ndtri(np.clip(uniform, 1.0e-14, 1.0 - 1.0e-14))
+        y = normal[:, :d] / np.abs(normal[:, d, None])
+        y_norm_sq = np.sum(y * y, axis=1)
+
+        pair_masses = np.linspace(0.45 / d, 0.75 / d, d)
+        pair_masses *= 0.72 / np.sum(pair_masses)
+        radii = np.linspace(0.65, 2.15, d)
+        covariance = np.diag(pair_masses * radii**2)
+        trace = float(np.trace(covariance))
+        covariance_zero = covariance - trace * np.eye(d) / d
+        iso = 3 * (d + 1) * (7 * d + 9) / (
+            4 * d * (d + 3) * (d + 5) * (d + 7)
+        )
+        traceless = 3 * (d + 1) * (d + 3) / (4 * (d + 5) * (d + 7))
+        q_value = iso * trace**2 + traceless * float(
+            np.sum(covariance_zero * covariance_zero)
+        )
+
+        quadratic = np.einsum("ni,ij,nj->n", y, covariance, y)
+        b_mode = (d + 1) / 2 * (
+            -trace / (1 + y_norm_sq)
+            + (d + 3) * quadratic / (1 + y_norm_sq) ** 2
+        )
+
+        cross_ratios = []
+        scaled_defects = []
+        for time in times:
+            quotient = np.full(y.shape[0], 1.0 - np.sum(pair_masses))
+            for axis, (mass, radius) in enumerate(zip(pair_masses, radii)):
+                shift = radius / time
+                dot = y[:, axis]
+                plus_denominator = 1 + y_norm_sq + shift**2 - 2 * shift * dot
+                minus_denominator = 1 + y_norm_sq + shift**2 + 2 * shift * dot
+                quotient += 0.5 * mass * (
+                    ((1 + y_norm_sq) / plus_denominator) ** ((d + 1) / 2)
+                    + ((1 + y_norm_sq) / minus_denominator) ** ((d + 1) / 2)
+                )
+
+            delta = quotient - 1
+            u = b_mode / time**2
+            remainder = delta - u
+            proxy_entropy = np.mean(
+                (1 + u) * np.log1p(u) - u
+                + remainder * np.log1p(u)
+            )
+            proxy_divergence = np.mean(
+                (1 + delta) * np.log((1 + delta) / (1 + u))
+                - remainder
+            )
+            cross_ratios.append(time**4 * proxy_entropy / q_value)
+            scaled_defects.append(time**4 * proxy_divergence)
+
+        row_ok = (
+            all(np.isfinite(cross_ratios))
+            and all(np.isfinite(scaled_defects))
+            and all(value >= -2.0e-10 for value in scaled_defects)
+            and abs(cross_ratios[-1] - 1.0) < (0.03 if quick else 0.012)
+            and abs(cross_ratios[-1] - 1.0) < abs(cross_ratios[0] - 1.0)
+            and scaled_defects[-1] < scaled_defects[0]
+        )
+        passed = passed and row_ok
+        rows.append(
+            f"d={d}: t^4 cross/Q={cross_ratios}, "
+            f"t^4 D(h||k)={scaled_defects}"
+        )
+
+    return Check(
+        "finite-covariance proxy asymptotic stress",
+        passed,
+        "; ".join(rows),
+    )
+

+def covariance_proxy_quadrature_check(quick: bool) -> Check:
+    """Test the proxy chain identity and cross-entropy coefficient in d>=4."""
+
+    node_count = 70 if quick else 130
+    nodes, weights = leggauss(node_count)
+    theta = (math.pi / 4) * (nodes + 1)
+    theta_weights = (math.pi / 4) * weights
+    phi = (math.pi / 2) * (nodes + 1)
+    phi_weights = (math.pi / 2) * weights
+    radius_nodes = np.tan(theta)
+
+    rows = []
+    passed = True
+    times = (2.5, 4.0, 7.0, 12.0) if quick else (2.5, 4.0, 7.0, 12.0, 20.0)
+    for d, sphere_radius in ((4, 0.8), (7, 1.3), (11, 1.7)):
+        radial_weights = theta_weights * np.sin(theta) ** (d - 1)
+        radial_weights /= np.sum(radial_weights)
+        angular_weights = phi_weights * np.sin(phi) ** (d - 2)
+        angular_weights /= np.sum(angular_weights)
+        cosines = np.cos(phi)
+        exponent = (d + 1) / 2
+        variance = sphere_radius**2 / d
+        radius_squared = radius_nodes**2
+        b_sigma = exponent * (
+            -sphere_radius**2 / (1 + radius_squared)
+            + (d + 3)
+            * variance
+            * radius_squared
+            / (1 + radius_squared) ** 2
+        )
+        q_sigma = 0.5 * float(np.sum(radial_weights * b_sigma**2))
+
+        ratios = []
+        identity_errors = []
+        minimum_defect = math.inf
+        rejected_times = []
+        valid_times = []
+        for time in times:
+            epsilon = sphere_radius / time
+            denominator = (
+                1
+                + radius_squared[:, None]
+                + epsilon**2
+                - 2 * radius_nodes[:, None] * epsilon * cosines[None, :]
+            )
+            quotient_values = np.sum(
+                angular_weights[None, :]
+                * ((1 + radius_squared[:, None]) / denominator) ** exponent,
+                axis=1,
+            )
+            proxy_values = 1 + b_sigma / time**2
+            if np.min(proxy_values) <= 0:
+                rejected_times.append(time)
+                continue
+            valid_times.append(time)
+            entropy = float(
+                np.sum(
+                    radial_weights
+                    * quotient_values
+                    * np.log(quotient_values)
+                )
+            )
+            defect = float(
+                np.sum(
+                    radial_weights
+                    * quotient_values
+                    * np.log(quotient_values / proxy_values)
+                )
+            )
+            cross_entropy = float(
+                np.sum(
+                    radial_weights
+                    * quotient_values
+                    * np.log(proxy_values)
+                )
+            )
+            identity_errors.append(abs(entropy - defect - cross_entropy))
+            minimum_defect = min(minimum_defect, defect)
+            ratios.append(time**4 * cross_entropy / q_sigma)
+
+        row_ok = (
+            max(identity_errors) < 2.0e-13
+            and minimum_defect > -2.0e-13
+            and abs(ratios[-1] - 1) < (0.012 if quick else 0.005)
+            and abs(ratios[-1] - 1) < abs(ratios[0] - 1)
+        )
+        passed = passed and row_ok
+        rows.append(
+            f"d={d}, sphere radius={sphere_radius}: "
+            f"valid t={valid_times}, positivity-gate rejections={rejected_times}, "
+            f"t^4 cross/Q ratios={ratios}, "
+            f"max chain residual={max(identity_errors):.3e}, "
+            f"min proxy KL={minimum_defect:.3e}"
+        )
+    return Check(
+        "covariance proxy multidimensional quadrature",
+        passed,
+        "; ".join(rows),
+    )
+
+
 def quotient(y: mp.mpf, t: mp.mpf, law: Sequence[tuple[mp.mpf, mp.mpf]]) -> mp.mpf:
     return sum(weight * (1 + y * y) / (1 + (y - location / t) ** 2) for weight, location in law)

 succeeded in 608ms:
    1: % sec_introduction.tex -- Introduction
    2: \section{Introduction}\label{sec:introduction}
    3: 
    4: The Cayley transform
    5: \[
    6: \iota(x)=\frac{1+\ii x}{1-\ii x}
    7: \]
    8: identifies the one-point compactification of \(\RR\) with \(\TT\), and pulls
    9: normalized Haar measure back to the Cauchy law
   10: \[
   11: \omega(\dd x)=\frac{1}{\pi(1+x^{2})}\dd x=P_1(x)\dd x .
   12: \]
   13: This is the endpoint-normalized form of the classical Cauchy/wrapped-Cauchy
   14: M{\"o}bius correspondence in circular statistics
   15: \cite{KentTyler1988WrappedCauchy,McCullagh1992CauchyModels,
   16: McCullagh1996CauchyMobius,
   17: KatoJones2010MobiusCircle,KatoMcCullagh2020SphereCauchyMobius}.
   18: This normalization makes the fixed-law Poisson comparison
   19: \begin{equation}\label{eq:intro-main-functional}
   20: D_{\mathrm{KL}}\bigl(P_t*\nu\,\|\,P_t(\cdot-\bar{\gamma})\bigr)
   21: \end{equation}
   22: the object whose large-\(t\) behaviour is studied in Cayley--Chebyshev
   23: modes.  The input law \(\nu\) is fixed, \(\bar{\gamma}\) is its mean, and
   24: \(t\to\infty\) is the Poisson scale.
   25: 
   26: The basic calculation is a quotient calculation.  Let
   27: \[
   28: X_c=\gamma-\bar{\gamma}
   29: \]
   30: be the centred translation variable.  With \(x=\bar{\gamma}+ty\), translation
   31: by \(X_c\) is encoded by
   32: \[
   33: R_\varepsilon(y)=\frac{1+y^2}{1+(y-\varepsilon)^2},
   34: \qquad \varepsilon=X_c/t .
   35: \]
   36: The Cayley angle \(y=\tan\theta\) turns the Taylor coefficients of
   37: \(R_\varepsilon\) into finite trigonometric, equivalently Laurent, modes.
   38: After the matched Cauchy profile has been divided out, the quotient expansion
   39: is therefore reduced to finite mode algebra; the KL expansion is then the
   40: ordinary Taylor expansion of \((1+u)\log(1+u)-u\) in
   41: \(L^\infty\cap L^1(\omega)\).
   42: 
   43: \subsection*{Contribution}
   44: 
   45: The principal unconditional result is
   46: Theorem~\ref{thm:sharp-leading-kl-variance-threshold}.  For every law with
   47: finite mean and without a tail-class assumption, finite centred variance is
   48: equivalent to bounded \(t^4D_{\rm KL}\); the limit is \(\mu_2^2/8\) in the
   49: finite-variance case and \(+\infty\) otherwise.  The infinite-variance proof
   50: pairs the quotient with the bounded second Cayley mode and therefore does not
   51: use a formal Taylor expansion.
   52: 
   53: The other principal analytic result is the regularly varying boundary theorem,
   54: Theorem~\ref{cor:main-body-conditional-missing-top-moment-tail}.  For \(N\ge3\),
   55: a centred law with two-sided regularly varying tails of index \(p=2N-2\) and
   56: divergent integrated slowly varying factor
   57: \(\ell_L(t)=\int^tL(s)\dd s/s\) satisfies
   58: \[
   59: t^{2N}\left\{H(t)-\sum_{j=2}^{N-1}A_{2j}(\nu)t^{-2j}\right\}
   60: \sim p(c_++c_-)\kappa_N\mu_2\ell_L(t),
   61: \qquad
   62: \kappa_N=(-1)^N(N-1)2^{-2N+2}.
   63: \]
   64: Equivalently, the residual normalized by the truncated boundary moment has
   65: limit \(\kappa_N\mu_2\).  This is a class-conditional analytic statement,
   66: without a density, symmetry, or monotonicity assumption; it is not an
   67: unconditional higher-order converse.
   68: 
   69: The all-order coefficient algebra has a different logical status.  For every
   70: \(m\ge3\), the reduced universal polynomial satisfies
   71: \[
   72: A_{2m}^{\rm form}
   73: =\kappa_m\mathfrak m_2\mathfrak m_{2m-2}
   74: +P_m(\mathfrak m_2,\ldots,\mathfrak m_{2m-3}),
   75: \qquad \kappa_m=(-1)^m(m-1)2^{-2m+2}\ne0.
   76: \]
   77: At \(m=2\), one instead has \(A_4^{\rm form}=\mathfrak m_2^2/8\).
   78: Thus \(\EE|X_c|^{2m-2}<\infty\) is sufficient to evaluate every monomial and
   79: is the minimal absolute-moment assumption that guarantees such evaluation
   80: uniformly over all centred laws.  Proposition~\ref{thm:sharp-moment-equivalence-hierarchy}
   81: is only this algebraic polynomial-domain minimality statement.  In particular,
   82: for \(m\ge3\) it is not an unconditional converse for the entropy remainder.
   83: 
   84: The second theorem is multidimensional and finite-moment.  For
   85: \(L\ge d+1\), finite centred \(L\)-moment closes the large-translation
   86: remainder in the matched radial Poisson quotient, gives an explicit
   87: \(L^\infty\cap L^1(\Omega_d)\) jet, supplies the large-\(t\)
   88: entropy-existence package, and hence gives the tensor KL expansion through
   89: order \(L+2\).  The two-region expression is retained as the quantitative
   90: remainder bound; in this range the precise statement is the equivalence
   91: \(\mathcal R_{d,L}(t)=o(t^{-L})\) for the displayed functional
   92: \eqref{eq:conditional-multidim-two-region-tail} and the finite
   93: \(L\)-moment assumption.
   94: The restriction \(L\ge d+1\) is sharp for this \(L^\infty\) quotient
   95: topology, as shown by the spike construction below.  It is not claimed to be
   96: necessary for the leading KL coefficient in a weaker \(L^2(\Omega_d)\) or
   97: entropy topology.  Section~\ref{sec:verified-A2-results} treats those weaker
   98: topologies separately: the sharp moment exponent for the \(L^2\) quotient is
   99: \(\max\{2,(d+1)/2\}\); finite covariance is the exact leading-KL threshold
  100: for \(d\le3\); and finite covariance fails to control the leading KL scale
  101: for every \(d\ge4\).  In the latter dimensions the exact exponent among
  102: finite absolute-moment hypotheses is \(4(d+1)/(d+5)\), with the critical
  103: endpoint on the sufficient side; see
  104: Theorem~\ref{thm:high-dimensional-kl-moment-threshold}.  The infinite
  105: critical-moment boundary is genuinely different: Theorem
  106: \ref{thm:critical-vague-tail-nonidentifiability} constructs two laws with the
  107: same smooth multivariate regularly varying tail data and the same covariance,
  108: one attaining the covariance coefficient and the other having unbounded
  109: scaled entropy along a subsequence.
  110: Theorem~\ref{thm:covariance-proxy-defect-decomposition} gives an exact
  111: finite-covariance characterization of this distinction.  If
  112: \(k_{\Sigma,t}\) denotes the positive Poisson density obtained by adjoining
  113: the normalized quadratic mode \(t^{-2}b_\Sigma\) to the matched reference,
  114: then
  115: \[
  116:  t^4H_d(t)=\mathcal Q_d(\Sigma)
  117:  +t^4D_{\rm KL}(h_t\|k_{\Sigma,t})+o(1).
  118: \]
  119: Thus attainment of the covariance coefficient is equivalent to the
  120: vanishing of the complete nonlinear proxy defect.  This statement requires
  121: only finite covariance and does not impose a density or tail regularity on
  122: the original law.
  123: The leading multidimensional covariance formula is deliberately read through
  124: this analytic route.  Finite covariance alone determines the bounded quadratic
  125: mode \(b_\Sigma\) and the algebraic number \(\mathcal Q_d(\Sigma)\), but it
  126: does not by itself make \(\mathcal Q_d(\Sigma)\) the coefficient of
  127: \eqref{eq:intro-main-functional}.  It becomes the leading KL coefficient only
  128: after a quotient/existence bridge is in force, for example the
  129: \((\mathrm{MPJ}_{d,L})+(\mathrm{EX}_{d,L})\) package supplied here by
  130: finite \(L\)-moment with \(L\ge d+1\), or the separate second-order quotient
  131: criterion stated in Section~\ref{sec:entropy-asymptotics}.
  132: 
  133: The third theorem is a domain-and-endpoint result for the standard
  134: simultaneous-semigroup relative-entropy calculus.  For a symmetric jump
  135: generator
  136: \[
  137: (L_Jf)(x)=\int\{f(y)-f(x)\}J(x,y)\,\dd y,
  138: \qquad J(x,y)=J(y,x),
  139: \]
  140: and sufficiently regular positive solutions
  141: \(\partial_t h_t=L_Jh_t\), \(\partial_t g_t=L_Jg_t\), set
  142: \(u_t=h_t/g_t\).  The general generator identity is
  143: \begin{equation}\label{eq:intro-general-two-solution-generator-identity}
  144: -\frac{\dd}{\dd t}D_{\rm KL}(h_t\|g_t)
  145: =\frac12\iint J(x,y)
  146: \left[g_t(x)\Lambda(u_t(x),u_t(y))
  147: +g_t(y)\Lambda(u_t(y),u_t(x))\right]\dd x\dd y.
  148: \end{equation}
  149: By exchanging \(x\) and \(y\) in the second term, the right side equals
  150: \[

   53: \author{Anonymous author}

   76: 
   77: \begin{document}
   78: \begin{abstract}
   79: For a fixed probability law~\(\nu\) on \(\RR\) with mean \(\bar{\gamma}\), the
   80: Cayley transform reduces the large-scale matched Poisson entropy
   81: \[
   82:   D_{\mathrm{KL}}(P_t*\nu\,\|\,P_t(\cdot-\bar{\gamma}))
   83: \]
   84:   to finite Laurent constant-term algebra.  The principal unconditional
   85:   result is the analytic variance threshold
   86:   \[
   87:   \mathbb E X_c^2<\infty
   88:   \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
   89:   \limsup_{t\to\infty}t^4
   90:   D_{\mathrm{KL}}(P_t*\nu\,\|\,P_t(\cdot-\bar\gamma))<\infty .
   91:   \]
   92:   The limit is \((\mathbb E X_c^2)^2/8\) when the variance is finite and is
   93:   \(+\infty\) otherwise; the infinite-variance direction uses a bounded
   94:   Cayley-mode test and no tail-class assumption.  At each higher boundary,
   95:   a second principal result gives a class-conditional analytic statement.
   96:   If the centred law has two-sided regularly varying tails of index
   97:   \(-(2N-2)\), with common slowly varying factor \(L\) and
   98:   \(\ell_L(t)=\int^tL(s)\dd s/s\to\infty\), then
   99:   \[
  100:   \frac{t^{2N}}{M_{2N-2}(t)}
  101:   \left\{D_{\mathrm{KL}}(P_t*\nu\,\|\,P_t)
  102:   -\sum_{j=2}^{N-1}A_{2j}(\nu)t^{-2j}\right\}
  103:   \longrightarrow \kappa_N\mu_2,
  104:   \]
  105:   where \(M_{2N-2}\) is the truncated boundary moment and
  106:   \(\kappa_N=(-1)^N(N-1)2^{-2N+2}\).  This regularly varying boundary
  107:   replacement permits asymmetric laws and requires neither a density nor
  108:   monotonicity of \(L\).
  109: 
  110: As supporting machinery, if \(\nu\) has finite centred
  111: \((2N-2)\)th absolute moment, then
  112: \[
  113: D_{\mathrm{KL}}(P_t*\nu\,\|\,P_t(\cdot-\bar{\gamma}))
  114: =\sum_{m=2}^{N}A_{2m}(\nu)t^{-2m}+o(t^{-2N}),
  115: \]
  116:   where the universal polynomials \(A_{2m}\) are obtained from the
  117:   Cayley--Chebyshev modes by one constant-term rule, and all odd coefficients
  118:   vanish.  For \(m\ge3\), algebraically,
  119:   \[
  120:   A_{2m}^{\rm form}=\kappa_m\mathfrak m_2\mathfrak m_{2m-2}
  121:   +P_m(\mathfrak m_2,\ldots,\mathfrak m_{2m-3}).
  122:   \]
  123:   At the leading order, \(A_4^{\rm form}=\mathfrak m_2^2/8\).
  124:   Consequently, finite \((2m-2)\)nd absolute moment is the minimal assumption
  125:   that guarantees evaluation of this universal polynomial over all centred
  126:   laws.  For \(m\ge3\) this is an algebraic polynomial-domain minimality
  127:   statement, not an unconditional analytic equivalence for the entropy
  128:   remainder.
  129: 
  130: In \(\RR^d\), finite \(L\)-moment with \(L\ge d+1\) gives a matched radial
  131:   Poisson quotient with an explicit two-region \(L^\infty\cap L^1(\Omega_d)\)
  132:   remainder and hence tensor KL coefficients through order \(L+2\); the
  133:   \(\mathcal R_{d,L}(t)=o(t^{-L})\) decay of the displayed
  134:   two-region
  135:   functional is equivalent to that finite-moment hypothesis in this range.
  136:   The threshold \(L\ge d+1\) is sharp for this uniform quotient topology;
  137:   the sharp all-class \(L^2(\Omega_d)\) moment exponent is instead
  138:   \(\max\{2,(d+1)/2\}\).  Finite covariance is necessary and sufficient for
  139:   the leading KL coefficient in dimensions \(d\le3\), whereas in every
  140:   \(d\ge4\) a finite positive-definite covariance can coexist with unbounded
  141:   \(t^4D_{\rm KL}\).  For \(d\ge4\), the sharp high-dimensional exponent among
  142:   finite absolute-moment hypotheses is
  143:   \(4(d+1)/(d+5)\), including sufficiency at the critical endpoint.
  144:   At the corresponding infinite-moment boundary, however, vague
  145:   multivariate regular variation does not determine the leading entropy:
  146:   two laws with the same smooth spectral measure, slowly varying factor,
  147:   and positive-definite covariance can respectively attain the covariance
  148:   coefficient and have unbounded \(t^4D_{\rm KL}\) along a subsequence.
  149:   For every finite covariance, a covariance-matched Poisson proxy isolates
  150:   this failure in an exact nonnegative defect:
  151:   \(t^4H_d(t)=\mathcal Q_d(\Sigma)
  152:   +t^4D_{\rm KL}(h_t\|k_{\Sigma,t})+o(1)\).
  153:   In one dimension, the first Cayley channel satisfies an exact
  154:   cross-scale differential closure.  Comparing against the \(n\)-node
  155:   Gaussian quadrature law gives an unconditional \(2n\)th-moment entropy
  156:   threshold and a positive square boundary under regular variation.
  157:   Under finite centred second moment, the
  158:   moving Cauchy quotient is verified to lie in the domain of the general
  159:   simultaneous-semigroup relative-entropy dissipation identity and satisfies
  160:   \[
  161:   D_{\mathrm{KL}}(P_t*\nu\,\|\,P_t(\cdot-\bar{\gamma}))
  162:   =\int_t^\infty \mathcal I_\nu(s)\,\dd s,
  163:   \]
  164:   after a separate endpoint passage.  Appendices collect related-work
  165: comparisons and auxiliary coefficient-calculus and tail-verification checks;
  166: they are not additional hypotheses for the main theorems.
  167: \end{abstract}

+            proxy_ok,
+            "; ".join(proxy_rows),
+        )
+    )
+
+    delta_symbol, u_symbol, log_delta, log_u = sp.symbols(
+        "delta u log_delta log_u"
+    )
+    chain_residual = sp.expand(
+        (1 + delta_symbol) * log_delta
+        - (1 + delta_symbol) * (log_delta - log_u)
+        - (1 + delta_symbol) * log_u
+    )
+    checks.append(
+        Check(
+            "covariance proxy exact KL chain identity",
+            chain_residual == 0,
+            f"pointwise residual={chain_residual}",
+        )
+    )
     return checks
 
 
+def finite_covariance_proxy_check(quick: bool) -> Check:
+    """Stress the proxy cross-entropy asymptotic on anisotropic atomic laws."""
+
+    rows = []
+    passed = True
+    sample_power = 18
+    times = (4.0, 7.0, 12.0) if quick else (4.0, 7.0, 12.0, 20.0)
+
+    for d in (4, 7):
+        sobol = qmc.Sobol(d=d + 1, scramble=True, seed=1729 + d)
+        uniform = sobol.random_base2(sample_power)
+        normal = ndtri(np.clip(uniform, 1.0e-14, 1.0 - 1.0e-14))
+        y = normal[:, :d] / np.abs(normal[:, d, None])
+        y_norm_sq = np.sum(y * y, axis=1)
+
+        pair_masses = np.linspace(0.45 / d, 0.75 / d, d)
+        pair_masses *= 0.72 / np.sum(pair_masses)
+        radii = np.linspace(0.65, 2.15, d)
+        covariance = np.diag(pair_masses * radii**2)
+        trace = float(np.trace(covariance))
+        covariance_zero = covariance - trace * np.eye(d) / d
+        iso = 3 * (d + 1) * (7 * d + 9) / (
+            4 * d * (d + 3) * (d + 5) * (d + 7)
+        )
+        traceless = 3 * (d + 1) * (d + 3) / (4 * (d + 5) * (d + 7))
+        q_value = iso * trace**2 + traceless * float(
+            np.sum(covariance_zero * covariance_zero)
+        )
+
+        quadratic = np.einsum("ni,ij,nj->n", y, covariance, y)
+        b_mode = (d + 1) / 2 * (
+            -trace / (1 + y_norm_sq)
+            + (d + 3) * quadratic / (1 + y_norm_sq) ** 2
+        )
+
+        cross_ratios = []
+        scaled_defects = []
+        for time in times:
+            quotient = np.full(y.shape[0], 1.0 - np.sum(pair_masses))
+            for axis, (mass, radius) in enumerate(zip(pair_masses, radii)):
+                shift = radius / time
+                dot = y[:, axis]
+                plus_denominator = 1 + y_norm_sq + shift**2 - 2 * shift * dot
+                minus_denominator = 1 + y_norm_sq + shift**2 + 2 * shift * dot
+                quotient += 0.5 * mass * (
+                    ((1 + y_norm_sq) / plus_denominator) ** ((d + 1) / 2)
+                    + ((1 + y_norm_sq) / minus_denominator) ** ((d + 1) / 2)
+                )
+
+            delta = quotient - 1
+            u = b_mode / time**2
+            remainder = delta - u
+            proxy_entropy = np.mean(
+                (1 + u) * np.log1p(u) - u
+                + remainder * np.log1p(u)
+            )
+            proxy_divergence = np.mean(
+                (1 + delta) * np.log((1 + delta) / (1 + u))
+                - remainder
+            )
+            cross_ratios.append(time**4 * proxy_entropy / q_value)
+            scaled_defects.append(time**4 * proxy_divergence)
+
+        row_ok = (
+            all(np.isfinite(cross_ratios))
+            and all(np.isfinite(scaled_defects))
+            and all(value >= -2.0e-10 for value in scaled_defects)
+            and abs(cross_ratios[-1] - 1.0) < (0.03 if quick else 0.012)
+            and abs(cross_ratios[-1] - 1.0) < abs(cross_ratios[0] - 1.0)
+            and scaled_defects[-1] < scaled_defects[0]
+        )
+        passed = passed and row_ok
+        rows.append(
+            f"d={d}: t^4 cross/Q={cross_ratios}, "
+            f"t^4 D(h||k)={scaled_defects}"
+        )
+
+    return Check(
+        "finite-covariance proxy asymptotic stress",
+        passed,
+        "; ".join(rows),
+    )
+
+

+def covariance_proxy_quadrature_check(quick: bool) -> Check:
+    """Test the proxy chain identity and cross-entropy coefficient in d>=4."""
+
+    node_count = 70 if quick else 130
+    nodes, weights = leggauss(node_count)
+    theta = (math.pi / 4) * (nodes + 1)
+    theta_weights = (math.pi / 4) * weights
+    phi = (math.pi / 2) * (nodes + 1)
+    phi_weights = (math.pi / 2) * weights
+    radius_nodes = np.tan(theta)
+
+    rows = []
+    passed = True
+    times = (2.5, 4.0, 7.0, 12.0) if quick else (2.5, 4.0, 7.0, 12.0, 20.0)
+    for d, sphere_radius in ((4, 0.8), (7, 1.3), (11, 1.7)):
+        radial_weights = theta_weights * np.sin(theta) ** (d - 1)
+        radial_weights /= np.sum(radial_weights)
+        angular_weights = phi_weights * np.sin(phi) ** (d - 2)
+        angular_weights /= np.sum(angular_weights)
+        cosines = np.cos(phi)
+        exponent = (d + 1) / 2
+        variance = sphere_radius**2 / d
+        radius_squared = radius_nodes**2
+        b_sigma = exponent * (
+            -sphere_radius**2 / (1 + radius_squared)
+            + (d + 3)
+            * variance
+            * radius_squared
+            / (1 + radius_squared) ** 2
+        )
+        q_sigma = 0.5 * float(np.sum(radial_weights * b_sigma**2))
+
+        ratios = []
+        identity_errors = []
+        minimum_defect = math.inf
+        rejected_times = []
+        valid_times = []
+        for time in times:
+            epsilon = sphere_radius / time
+            denominator = (
+                1
+                + radius_squared[:, None]
+                + epsilon**2
+                - 2 * radius_nodes[:, None] * epsilon * cosines[None, :]
+            )
+            quotient_values = np.sum(
+                angular_weights[None, :]
+                * ((1 + radius_squared[:, None]) / denominator) ** exponent,
+                axis=1,
+            )
+            proxy_values = 1 + b_sigma / time**2
+            if np.min(proxy_values) <= 0:
+                rejected_times.append(time)
+                continue
+            valid_times.append(time)
+            entropy = float(
+                np.sum(
+                    radial_weights
+                    * quotient_values
+                    * np.log(quotient_values)
+                )
+            )
+            defect = float(
+                np.sum(
+                    radial_weights
+                    * quotient_values
+                    * np.log(quotient_values / proxy_values)
+                )
+            )
+            cross_entropy = float(
+                np.sum(
+                    radial_weights
+                    * quotient_values
+                    * np.log(proxy_values)
+                )
+            )
+            identity_errors.append(abs(entropy - defect - cross_entropy))
+            minimum_defect = min(minimum_defect, defect)
+            ratios.append(time**4 * cross_entropy / q_sigma)
+
+        row_ok = (
+            max(identity_errors) < 2.0e-13
+            and minimum_defect > -2.0e-13
+            and abs(ratios[-1] - 1) < (0.012 if quick else 0.005)
+            and abs(ratios[-1] - 1) < abs(ratios[0] - 1)
+        )
+        passed = passed and row_ok
+        rows.append(
+            f"d={d}, sphere radius={sphere_radius}: "
+            f"valid t={valid_times}, positivity-gate rejections={rejected_times}, "
+            f"t^4 cross/Q ratios={ratios}, "
+            f"max chain residual={max(identity_errors):.3e}, "
+            f"min proxy KL={minimum_defect:.3e}"
+        )
+    return Check(
+        "covariance proxy multidimensional quadrature",
+        passed,
+        "; ".join(rows),
+    )
+
+
 def quotient(y: mp.mpf, t: mp.mpf, law: Sequence[tuple[mp.mpf, mp.mpf]]) -> mp.mpf:
     return sum(weight * (1 + y * y) / (1 + (y - location / t) ** 2) for weight, location in law)

    1: 你所提出的有限协方差 Poisson 代理缺陷分解，经独立重构后，其核心交叉熵估计可以成立；然而，关于“多个壳层在同一 Poisson 单元内聚合后不能通过壳层间抵消而规避检测”的表述不成立。设同一粗分割单元 (A=A_1\sqcup A_2)，代理质量分别为 (s_1,s_2)，而目标质量分别为 (p_1=s_1+\varepsilon)、(p_2=s_2-\varepsilon)。则 (p(A)=s(A))，故该粗单元对应的 Bernoulli KL 恰为零，尽管在细分 ({A_1,A_2}) 上的离散 KL 严格为正。请据此删除“同一单元内不可抵消”的断言，并给出严格的修正命题：只有沿生成 Borel (sigma)-代数的分割序列充分细化时，局部差异才由完整相对熵恢复；任何固定粗分割均可能掩盖子单元之间的符号相反偏差。此外，有限分割变分表示与 Bernoulli 数据处理下界属于经典相对熵理论，不得作为本文的新定理重复提出。

    1: % sec_covariance_proxy_defect.tex -- exact covariance-proxy KL defect
    2: \section{The Covariance-Matched Poisson Defect}
    3: \label{sec:covariance-proxy-defect}
    4: 
    5: Finite covariance determines the quadratic mode but, in dimensions at least
    6: four, does not force the matched entropy to attain the corresponding
    7: coefficient.  The following result identifies the complete nonnegative
    8: remainder without imposing a tail class or an absolute moment above order
    9: two.
   10: 
   11: \begin{theorem}[Exact covariance-proxy defect decomposition]
   12: \label{thm:covariance-proxy-defect-decomposition}
   13: \label{thm:finite-covariance-proxy-defect-decomposition}
   14: Let (d\ge1), and let \(\nu\) be a probability law on \(\RR^d\) with mean
   15: \(\bar\gamma\) and finite covariance matrix \(\Sigma\).  Put
   16: \[
   17:  h_t=P_t^{(d)}*\nu,
   18:  \qquad g_t=P_t^{(d)}(\mathord\cdot-\bar\gamma),
   19:  \qquad H_d(t)=D_{\rm KL}(h_t\|g_t),
   20: \]
   21: and let \(b_\Sigma\) be the bounded covariance mode in
   22: \eqref{eq:multidim-leading-theorem-density-mode}.  For all sufficiently
   23: large \(t\),
   24: \begin{equation}\label{eq:covariance-poisson-proxy}
   25:  k_{\Sigma,t}(x)
   26:  :=g_t(x)\left[1+t^{-2}b_\Sigma
   27:  \left(\frac{x-\bar\gamma}{t}\right)\right]
   28: \end{equation}
   29: is a probability density.  Define
   30: \begin{equation}\label{eq:covariance-proxy-defect-functional}
   31:  \mathcal C_{\nu,\Sigma}(t)
   32:  :=t^4D_{\rm KL}(h_t\|k_{\Sigma,t}).
   33: \end{equation}
   34: Then
   35: \begin{equation}\label{eq:covariance-proxy-defect-decomposition}
   36:  t^4H_d(t)
   37:  =\mathcal Q_d(\Sigma)+\mathcal C_{\nu,\Sigma}(t)+o(1)
   38:  \qquad(t\to\infty).
   39: \end{equation}
   40: Consequently,
   41: \begin{equation}\label{eq:covariance-proxy-defect-equivalence}
   42:  t^4H_d(t)\longrightarrow\mathcal Q_d(\Sigma)
   43:  \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
   44:  \mathcal C_{\nu,\Sigma}(t)\longrightarrow0.
   45: \end{equation}
   46: No density of \(\nu\), regular-variation hypothesis, angular balance, or
   47: higher absolute moment is required.
   48: \end{theorem}
   49: 
   50: \begin{proof}
   51: After translation, assume \(\bar\gamma=0\), and write
   52: \[
   53:  \Omega_d(\dd y)=P_1^{(d)}(y)\dd y,
   54:  \qquad
   55:  F_y(z)=\left(\frac{1+|y|^2}{1+|y-z|^2}\right)^{(d+1)/2}.
   56: \]
   57: In normalized coordinates,
   58: \[
   59:  q_t(y):=\frac{h_t(ty)}{g_t(ty)}
   60:  =\EE F_y(X/t),
   61:  \qquad \delta_t=q_t-1,
   62:  \qquad u_t=t^{-2}b_\Sigma.
   63: \]
   64: The covariance calculation in
   65: Theorem~\ref{thm:multidim-covariance-algebra} gives
   66: \(\int b_\Sigma\dd\Omega_d=0\).  Since \(b_\Sigma\) is bounded,
   67: \(1+u_t\ge1/2\) for all sufficiently large \(t\), proving that
   68: \eqref{eq:covariance-poisson-proxy} is positive and has integral one.
   69: 
   70: We first establish the only asymptotic input needed below:
   71: \begin{equation}\label{eq:covariance-proxy-weak-pairing}
   72:  t^2\int_{\RR^d}b_\Sigma(y)\delta_t(y)\,\Omega_d(\dd y)
   73:  \longrightarrow
   74:  \int_{\RR^d}b_\Sigma(y)^2\,\Omega_d(\dd y).
   75: \end{equation}
   76: Set
   77: \[
   78:  J(z)=\int_{\RR^d}b_\Sigma(y)F_y(z)\,\Omega_d(\dd y)
   79:  =\int_{\RR^d}b_\Sigma(y)P_1^{(d)}(y-z)\dd y.
   80: \]
   81: The function \(b_\Sigma\) is even, smooth, and has bounded second
   82: derivatives.  Translation in the last integral shows that \(J\) is even,
   83: \(J(0)=0\), and its Hessian is bounded on the unit ball.  Hence
   84: \(|J(z)|\le C|z|^2\) for \(|z|\le1\).  For \(|z|\ge1\), positivity and
   85: normalization of the translated Poisson density give
   86: \(|J(z)|\le\|b_\Sigma\|_\infty\le
   87: \|b_\Sigma\|_\infty|z|^2\).  Thus
   88: \begin{equation}\label{eq:covariance-proxy-global-quadratic-pairing}
   89:  |J(z)|\le C_{d,\Sigma}|z|^2,
   90:  \qquad z\in\RR^d.
   91: \end{equation}
   92: 
   93: Let \(B_2(y,z)\) denote the homogeneous degree-two Taylor term of
   94: \(F_y(z)\) at zero.  Taylor expansion under the integral gives
   95: \[
   96:  J(z)=\int b_\Sigma(y)B_2(y,z)\,\Omega_d(\dd y)+o(|z|^2).
   97: \]
   98: The covariance-mode identity proved in
   99: Theorem~\ref{thm:multidim-covariance-algebra} is
  100: \(\EE B_2(y,X)=b_\Sigma(y)\).  Since \(\EE|X|^2<\infty\),
  101: \eqref{eq:covariance-proxy-global-quadratic-pairing} and dominated
  102: convergence imply
  103: \[
  104:  \EE[t^2J(X/t)]
  105:  \longrightarrow
  106:  \int b_\Sigma(y)\EE B_2(y,X)\,\Omega_d(\dd y)
  107:  =\int b_\Sigma^2\dd\Omega_d.
  108: \]
  109: Because \(\int b_\Sigma\dd\Omega_d=0\), the left side is precisely the
  110: left side of \eqref{eq:covariance-proxy-weak-pairing}.
  111: 
  112: We also have
  113: \begin{equation}\label{eq:covariance-proxy-l1-smallness}
  114:  \|\delta_t\|_{L^1(\Omega_d)}\longrightarrow0.
  115: \end{equation}
  116: Indeed, Minkowski's integral inequality bounds this norm by
  117: \[
  118:  \EE\left\|P_1^{(d)}(\mathord\cdot-X/t)-P_1^{(d)}
  119:  \right\|_{L^1(\RR^d)},
  120: \]
  121: which tends to zero by translation continuity and dominated convergence.
  122: 
  123: Put \(r_t=\delta_t-u_t\).  Equations
  124: \eqref{eq:covariance-proxy-weak-pairing} and
  125: \eqref{eq:covariance-proxy-l1-smallness} give
  126: \begin{equation}\label{eq:covariance-proxy-remainder-pairing}
  127:  t^2\int b_\Sigma r_t\dd\Omega_d\longrightarrow0,
  128:  \qquad \|r_t\|_{L^1(\Omega_d)}\longrightarrow0.
  129: \end{equation}
  130: Uniformly in \(y\),
  131: \[
  132:  \log(1+u_t)=u_t+O(t^{-4}).
  133: \]
  134: It follows from \eqref{eq:covariance-proxy-remainder-pairing} that
  135: \begin{equation}\label{eq:covariance-proxy-cross-remainder}
  136:  \int r_t\log(1+u_t)\dd\Omega_d=o(t^{-4}).
  137: \end{equation}
  138: On the other hand, with
  139: \(\Phi(s)=(1+s)\log(1+s)-s\), boundedness of \(b_\Sigma\) gives
  140: \begin{align}
  141:  \int(1+u_t)\log(1+u_t)\dd\Omega_d
  142:  &=\int\Phi(u_t)\dd\Omega_d \notag\\
  143:  &=\frac{t^{-4}}2\int b_\Sigma^2\dd\Omega_d+O(t^{-6})
  144:  =t^{-4}\mathcal Q_d(\Sigma)+O(t^{-6}).
  145:  \label{eq:covariance-proxy-self-cross-entropy}
  146: \end{align}
  147: Combining \eqref{eq:covariance-proxy-cross-remainder} and
  148: \eqref{eq:covariance-proxy-self-cross-entropy} yields
  149: \begin{equation}\label{eq:covariance-proxy-cross-entropy-asymptotic}
  150:  \int(1+\delta_t)\log(1+u_t)\dd\Omega_d
  151:  =t^{-4}\mathcal Q_d(\Sigma)+o(t^{-4}).
  152: \end{equation}
  153: 
  154: Finally, the exact logarithmic identity is
  155: \[
  156:  H_d(t)=D_{\rm KL}(h_t\|k_{\Sigma,t})
  157:  +\int(1+\delta_t)\log(1+u_t)\dd\Omega_d.
  158: \]
  159: All terms are finite for large \(t\): convexity bounds \(H_d(t)\) by the
  160: average relative entropy of translated Poisson kernels, which is integrable
  161: under \(\EE|X|^2<\infty\), and \(\log(1+u_t)\) is bounded.  Substitution of
  162: \eqref{eq:covariance-proxy-cross-entropy-asymptotic} proves
  163: \eqref{eq:covariance-proxy-defect-decomposition}.  Nonnegativity of relative
  164: entropy then gives \eqref{eq:covariance-proxy-defect-equivalence}.
  165: \end{proof}
  166: 
  167: \begin{corollary}[Proxy separation at the critical vague boundary]
  168: \label{cor:critical-rv-proxy-characterization}
  169: For the two laws \(\nu_{\rm ac}\) and \(\nu_{\rm cl}\) in
  170: Theorem~\ref{thm:critical-vague-tail-nonidentifiability}, which have the same
  171: critical vague tail limit and the same covariance \(\Sigma\),
  172: \[
  173:  \mathcal C_{\nu_{\rm ac},\Sigma}(t)\longrightarrow0,
  174:  \qquad
  175:  \limsup_{t\to\infty}\mathcal C_{\nu_{\rm cl},\Sigma}(t)=+\infty.
  176: \]
  177: Thus vague regular variation, including its spectral measure and slowly
  178: varying factor, does not determine the covariance-proxy defect trajectory.
  179: \end{corollary}
  180: 
  181: \begin{proof}
  182: Combine Theorem~\ref{thm:covariance-proxy-defect-decomposition} with the two
  183: entropy conclusions in
  184: \eqref{eq:critical-vague-tail-contrasting-entropy}.
  185: \end{proof}

# Literature Check: Regularly Varying Poisson-Entropy Boundary Layer

Checked 2026-08-02 (Asia/Singapore). The search target was the
coefficient-subtracted large-scale asymptotic

\[
t^{2N}\left\{
D_{\mathrm{KL}}(P_t*\nu\Vert P_t)
-\sum_{j=2}^{N-1}A_{2j}(\nu)t^{-2j}
\right\}
\]

for a fixed centred law whose tail is regularly varying with the exact
missing-moment index \(2N-2\).

## arXiv API Queries

The following requests were made directly to the required HTTP API endpoint.
The returned Atom feeds were inspected through their
`opensearch:totalResults` fields and, for nonempty feeds, through their
entry titles and abstracts.

| Query | Results | Relevance finding |
|---|---:|---|
| `http://export.arxiv.org/api/query?search_query=all:%22Poisson%20kernel%22%20AND%20all:%22relative%20entropy%22%20AND%20all:%22regular%20variation%22&start=0&max_results=25` | 0 | No direct match. |
| `http://export.arxiv.org/api/query?search_query=all:%22Cauchy%20semigroup%22%20AND%20all:entropy%20AND%20all:asymptotic&start=0&max_results=25` | 0 | No direct match. |
| `http://export.arxiv.org/api/query?search_query=all:%22regularly%20varying%22%20AND%20all:%22relative%20entropy%22&start=0&max_results=25` | 0 | No direct match. |
| `http://export.arxiv.org/api/query?search_query=all:%22Poisson%20semigroup%22%20AND%20all:%22entropy%22&start=0&max_results=25` | 0 | No direct match. |
| `http://export.arxiv.org/api/query?search_query=all:%22truncated%20moment%22%20AND%20all:%22Poisson%20kernel%22%20AND%20all:entropy&start=0&max_results=50` | 0 | No direct match. |
| `http://export.arxiv.org/api/query?search_query=all:%22regular%20variation%22%20AND%20all:%22Cauchy%22%20AND%20all:%22relative%20entropy%22&start=0&max_results=50` | 0 | No direct match. |
| `http://export.arxiv.org/api/query?search_query=all:%22slowly%20varying%22%20AND%20all:%22Poisson%20semigroup%22%20AND%20all:entropy&start=0&max_results=50` | 0 | No direct match. |
| `http://export.arxiv.org/api/query?search_query=all:%22stable%20law%22%20AND%20all:%22relative%20entropy%22&start=0&max_results=25` | 4 | Relevant comparators, but none studies the fixed-law, moving-Cauchy-reference, coefficient-subtracted boundary residual. |
| `http://export.arxiv.org/api/query?search_query=all:%22Hardy-Stein%22&start=0&max_results=50` | 14 | Structural semigroup/Bregman comparators; no matching boundary-layer theorem. |

The nonempty stable-law query returned, in particular:

- S. G. Bobkov, G. P. Chistyakov, and F. Goetze, *Convergence to Stable
  Laws in Relative Entropy*, arXiv:1104.4360,
  DOI 10.1007/s10959-011-0377-0.
- Oliver Johnson, *A de Bruijn identity for symmetric stable laws*,
  arXiv:1310.2045. No DOI was present in the arXiv or Crossref records
  located.
- Giuseppe Toscani, *Entropy Inequalities for Stable Densities and
  Strengthened Central Limit Theorems*, arXiv:1512.05874,
  DOI 10.1007/s10955-016-1619-4.

The Hardy--Stein query returned the principal comparators:

  *Hardy--Stein identities and square functions for semigroups*,
  arXiv:1506.09007, DOI 10.1112/jlms/jdw042.

  multipliers*, arXiv:1702.06573,
  DOI 10.1016/j.jmaa.2019.123383.

## Standard External Inputs

The new proof invokes only the regular-variation results in the first item
below. The remaining items are exact contextual comparators; their theorems
are not imported into the boundary-layer proof.

- N. H. Bingham, C. M. Goldie, and J. L. Teugels,
  *Regular Variation*, Cambridge University Press, 1987,
  DOI 10.1017/CBO9780511721434. Proposition 1.5.9a supplies the divergent
  integrated-slow-variation fact
  \(L(t)=o(\int^tL(s)\,ds/s)\); Theorem 1.5.11 supplies Karamata's
  direct and tail integral theorems. This is the only external theorem used
  in the new proof.
- Laurens de Haan and Ana Ferreira, *Extreme Value Theory: An
  Introduction*, Springer, 2006, DOI 10.1007/0-387-34471-3. This records the
  de Haan/second-order regular-variation framework. No second-order theorem
  from this source is used; the paper explicitly leaves second-order entropy
  corrections open.
- N. G. de Bruijn, *A property of heat equation connected with Shannon's
  theory of information*, Nederl. Akad. Wetensch. Proc. Ser. A 56 (1953),
  80-81. No DOI or arXiv identifier was found. This is historical context,
  not a proof input.
- The Hardy--Stein and stable-entropy papers listed above are contextual
  comparators, not proof inputs.

DOI metadata for the books and journal articles was independently checked
against the Crossref REST endpoint
`https://api.crossref.org/works/<DOI>`.

## Novelty Determination

No arXiv record located by the targeted or comparator queries states the
following combination: a fixed centred input law, harmonic Poisson/Cauchy
smoothing, subtraction of all finite Cayley--entropy coefficients below the
missing moment, a tail index exactly \(2N-2\), and the explicit limit
normalized by
\(\ell_L(t)=\int^tL(s)\,ds/s\).

The nearest results separate into three established bodies of work:
Karamata/de Haan regular-variation theory; de Bruijn or Hardy--Stein entropy
dissipation identities; and entropic convergence to stable laws. The
manuscript cites those inputs and does not claim any of their theorems as
new. Its new statement is the quotient-to-entropy synthesis yielding the
constant
\[
p(c_++c_-)(-1)^N(N-1)2^{-2N+2}\mu_2.
\]

This is a documented negative literature search, not a logically exhaustive
proof that no publication exists outside the indexed sources. Within the
queried arXiv corpus and the standard references/comparators above, no prior
publication of the stated theorem was found.

## Covariance-Proxy Defect Search

Checked 2026-08-08 (Asia/Singapore).  The additional search target was an
exact large-scale decomposition of radial Poisson relative entropy into the
quadratic covariance coefficient and the relative entropy from a
covariance-matched second-order Poisson proxy.

Direct arXiv API queries gave the following results:

| Query | Results | Relevance finding |
|---|---:|---|
| `all:"Poisson kernel" AND all:"relative entropy" AND all:covariance` | 0 | No matching record. |
| `all:"Cauchy semigroup" AND all:"relative entropy"` | 0 | No matching record. |
| `all:"Poisson semigroup" AND all:entropy AND all:deficit` | 0 | No matching record. |
| `all:"Poisson kernel" AND all:"Kullback-Leibler"` | 2 | The returned path-planning and discrete-kernel-smoothing papers are unrelated. |
| `all:Poisson AND all:"relative entropy"` | 10 in the requested page | The results concern Poisson channels, Poisson approximation, coding, Euler--Poisson systems, or Poisson suspensions, not harmonic Poisson smoothing against a covariance proxy. |

A Crossref bibliographic search for `Poisson kernel relative entropy
covariance deficit` returned Poisson-channel and Poisson-approximation papers,
an ARMA covariance-constraint paper, and unrelated applications.  None states
the covariance-proxy decomposition.  The closest standard information-theory
inputs remain the classical data-processing and finite-partition
characterizations of relative entropy; those facts are not claimed as new.

No indexed source located in this search gives the combination of a fixed
input law with only finite covariance, harmonic Poisson smoothing, the
explicit bounded mode (b_\Sigma), and the asymptotic identity

\[
t^4D_{\rm KL}(h_t\Vert g_t)
=\mathcal Q_d(\Sigma)+t^4D_{\rm KL}(h_t\Vert k_{\Sigma,t})+o(1).
\]

As above, this is a documented negative search rather than a logically
exhaustive proof of absence from all literature.

+def finite_covariance_proxy_check(quick: bool) -> Check:
+    """Stress the proxy cross-entropy asymptotic on anisotropic atomic laws."""
+
+    rows = []
+    passed = True
+    sample_power = 18
+    times = (4.0, 7.0, 12.0) if quick else (4.0, 7.0, 12.0, 20.0)
+
+    for d in (4, 7):
+        sobol = qmc.Sobol(d=d + 1, scramble=True, seed=1729 + d)
+        uniform = sobol.random_base2(sample_power)
+        normal = ndtri(np.clip(uniform, 1.0e-14, 1.0 - 1.0e-14))
+        y = normal[:, :d] / np.abs(normal[:, d, None])
+        y_norm_sq = np.sum(y * y, axis=1)
+
+        pair_masses = np.linspace(0.45 / d, 0.75 / d, d)
+        pair_masses *= 0.72 / np.sum(pair_masses)
+        radii = np.linspace(0.65, 2.15, d)
+        covariance = np.diag(pair_masses * radii**2)
+        trace = float(np.trace(covariance))
+        covariance_zero = covariance - trace * np.eye(d) / d
+        iso = 3 * (d + 1) * (7 * d + 9) / (
+            4 * d * (d + 3) * (d + 5) * (d + 7)
+        )
+        traceless = 3 * (d + 1) * (d + 3) / (4 * (d + 5) * (d + 7))
+        q_value = iso * trace**2 + traceless * float(
+            np.sum(covariance_zero * covariance_zero)
+        )
+
+        quadratic = np.einsum("ni,ij,nj->n", y, covariance, y)
+        b_mode = (d + 1) / 2 * (
+            -trace / (1 + y_norm_sq)
+            + (d + 3) * quadratic / (1 + y_norm_sq) ** 2
+        )
+
+        cross_ratios = []
+        scaled_defects = []
+        for time in times:
+            quotient = np.full(y.shape[0], 1.0 - np.sum(pair_masses))
+            for axis, (mass, radius) in enumerate(zip(pair_masses, radii)):
+                shift = radius / time
+                dot = y[:, axis]
+                plus_denominator = 1 + y_norm_sq + shift**2 - 2 * shift * dot
+                minus_denominator = 1 + y_norm_sq + shift**2 + 2 * shift * dot
+                quotient += 0.5 * mass * (
+                    ((1 + y_norm_sq) / plus_denominator) ** ((d + 1) / 2)
+                    + ((1 + y_norm_sq) / minus_denominator) ** ((d + 1) / 2)
+                )
+
+            delta = quotient - 1
+            u = b_mode / time**2
+            remainder = delta - u
+            proxy_entropy = np.mean(
+                (1 + u) * np.log1p(u) - u
+                + remainder * np.log1p(u)
+            )
+            proxy_divergence = np.mean(
+                (1 + delta) * np.log((1 + delta) / (1 + u))
+                - remainder
+            )
+            cross_ratios.append(time**4 * proxy_entropy / q_value)
+            scaled_defects.append(time**4 * proxy_divergence)
+
+        row_ok = (
+            all(np.isfinite(cross_ratios))
+            and all(np.isfinite(scaled_defects))
+            and all(value >= -2.0e-10 for value in scaled_defects)
+            and abs(cross_ratios[-1] - 1.0) < (0.03 if quick else 0.012)
+            and abs(cross_ratios[-1] - 1.0) < abs(cross_ratios[0] - 1.0)
+            and scaled_defects[-1] < scaled_defects[0]
+        )
+        passed = passed and row_ok
+        rows.append(
+            f"d={d}: t^4 cross/Q={cross_ratios}, "
+            f"t^4 D(h||k)={scaled_defects}"
+        )
+
+    return Check(
+        "finite-covariance proxy asymptotic stress",
+        passed,
+        "; ".join(rows),
+    )
+

+def covariance_proxy_quadrature_check(quick: bool) -> Check:
+    """Test the proxy chain identity and cross-entropy coefficient in d>=4."""
+
+    node_count = 70 if quick else 130
+    nodes, weights = leggauss(node_count)
+    theta = (math.pi / 4) * (nodes + 1)
+    theta_weights = (math.pi / 4) * weights
+    phi = (math.pi / 2) * (nodes + 1)
+    phi_weights = (math.pi / 2) * weights
+    radius_nodes = np.tan(theta)
+
+    rows = []
+    passed = True
+    times = (2.5, 4.0, 7.0, 12.0) if quick else (2.5, 4.0, 7.0, 12.0, 20.0)
+    for d, sphere_radius in ((4, 0.8), (7, 1.3), (11, 1.7)):
+        radial_weights = theta_weights * np.sin(theta) ** (d - 1)
+        radial_weights /= np.sum(radial_weights)
+        angular_weights = phi_weights * np.sin(phi) ** (d - 2)
+        angular_weights /= np.sum(angular_weights)
+        cosines = np.cos(phi)
+        exponent = (d + 1) / 2
+        variance = sphere_radius**2 / d
+        radius_squared = radius_nodes**2
+        b_sigma = exponent * (
+            -sphere_radius**2 / (1 + radius_squared)
+            + (d + 3)
+            * variance
+            * radius_squared
+            / (1 + radius_squared) ** 2
+        )
+        q_sigma = 0.5 * float(np.sum(radial_weights * b_sigma**2))
+
+        ratios = []
+        identity_errors = []
+        minimum_defect = math.inf
+        rejected_times = []
+        valid_times = []
+        for time in times:
+            epsilon = sphere_radius / time
+            denominator = (
+                1
+                + radius_squared[:, None]
+                + epsilon**2
+                - 2 * radius_nodes[:, None] * epsilon * cosines[None, :]
+            )
+            quotient_values = np.sum(
+                angular_weights[None, :]
+                * ((1 + radius_squared[:, None]) / denominator) ** exponent,
+                axis=1,
+            )
+            proxy_values = 1 + b_sigma / time**2
+            if np.min(proxy_values) <= 0:
+                rejected_times.append(time)
+                continue
+            valid_times.append(time)
+            entropy = float(
+                np.sum(
+                    radial_weights
+                    * quotient_values
+                    * np.log(quotient_values)
+                )
+            )
+            defect = float(
+                np.sum(
+                    radial_weights
+                    * quotient_values
+                    * np.log(quotient_values / proxy_values)
+                )
+            )
+            cross_entropy = float(
+                np.sum(
+                    radial_weights
+                    * quotient_values
+                    * np.log(proxy_values)
+                )
+            )
+            identity_errors.append(abs(entropy - defect - cross_entropy))

+            ratios.append(time**4 * cross_entropy / q_sigma)

+            and abs(ratios[-1] - 1) < abs(ratios[0] - 1)
+        )

+        )
+    return Check(
+        "covariance proxy multidimensional quadrature",
+        passed,
+        "; ".join(rows),
+    )

+def finite_covariance_proxy_check(quick: bool) -> Check:
+    """Stress the proxy cross-entropy asymptotic on anisotropic atomic laws."""
+
+    rows = []
+    passed = True
+    sample_power = 18
+    times = (4.0, 7.0, 12.0) if quick else (4.0, 7.0, 12.0, 20.0)
+
+    for d in (4, 7):
+        sobol = qmc.Sobol(d=d + 1, scramble=True, seed=1729 + d)
+        uniform = sobol.random_base2(sample_power)
+        normal = ndtri(np.clip(uniform, 1.0e-14, 1.0 - 1.0e-14))
+        y = normal[:, :d] / np.abs(normal[:, d, None])
+        y_norm_sq = np.sum(y * y, axis=1)
+
+        pair_masses = np.linspace(0.45 / d, 0.75 / d, d)
+        pair_masses *= 0.72 / np.sum(pair_masses)
+        radii = np.linspace(0.65, 2.15, d)
+        covariance = np.diag(pair_masses * radii**2)
+        trace = float(np.trace(covariance))
+        covariance_zero = covariance - trace * np.eye(d) / d
+        iso = 3 * (d + 1) * (7 * d + 9) / (
+            4 * d * (d + 3) * (d + 5) * (d + 7)
+        )
+        traceless = 3 * (d + 1) * (d + 3) / (4 * (d + 5) * (d + 7))
+        q_value = iso * trace**2 + traceless * float(
+            np.sum(covariance_zero * covariance_zero)
+        )
+
+        quadratic = np.einsum("ni,ij,nj->n", y, covariance, y)
+        b_mode = (d + 1) / 2 * (
+            -trace / (1 + y_norm_sq)
+            + (d + 3) * quadratic / (1 + y_norm_sq) ** 2
+        )
+
+        cross_ratios = []
+        scaled_defects = []
+        for time in times:
+            quotient = np.full(y.shape[0], 1.0 - np.sum(pair_masses))
+            for axis, (mass, radius) in enumerate(zip(pair_masses, radii)):
+                shift = radius / time
+                dot = y[:, axis]
+                plus_denominator = 1 + y_norm_sq + shift**2 - 2 * shift * dot
+                minus_denominator = 1 + y_norm_sq + shift**2 + 2 * shift * dot
+                quotient += 0.5 * mass * (
+                    ((1 + y_norm_sq) / plus_denominator) ** ((d + 1) / 2)
+                    + ((1 + y_norm_sq) / minus_denominator) ** ((d + 1) / 2)
+                )
+
+            delta = quotient - 1
+            u = b_mode / time**2
+            remainder = delta - u
+            proxy_entropy = np.mean(
+                (1 + u) * np.log1p(u) - u
+                + remainder * np.log1p(u)
+            )
+            proxy_divergence = np.mean(
+                (1 + delta) * np.log((1 + delta) / (1 + u))
+                - remainder
+            )
+            cross_ratios.append(time**4 * proxy_entropy / q_value)
+            scaled_defects.append(time**4 * proxy_divergence)
+
+        row_ok = (
+            all(np.isfinite(cross_ratios))
+            and all(np.isfinite(scaled_defects))
+            and all(value >= -2.0e-10 for value in scaled_defects)
+            and abs(cross_ratios[-1] - 1.0) < (0.03 if quick else 0.012)
+            and abs(cross_ratios[-1] - 1.0) < abs(cross_ratios[0] - 1.0)
+            and scaled_defects[-1] < scaled_defects[0]
+        )
+        passed = passed and row_ok
+        rows.append(
+            f"d={d}: t^4 cross/Q={cross_ratios}, "
+            f"t^4 D(h||k)={scaled_defects}"
+        )
+
+    return Check(
+        "finite-covariance proxy asymptotic stress",
+        passed,
+        "; ".join(rows),
+    )
+

+def covariance_proxy_quadrature_check(quick: bool) -> Check:
+    """Test the proxy chain identity and cross-entropy coefficient in d>=4."""
+
+    node_count = 70 if quick else 130
+    nodes, weights = leggauss(node_count)
+    theta = (math.pi / 4) * (nodes + 1)
+    theta_weights = (math.pi / 4) * weights
+    phi = (math.pi / 2) * (nodes + 1)
+    phi_weights = (math.pi / 2) * weights
+    radius_nodes = np.tan(theta)
+
+    rows = []
+    passed = True
+    times = (2.5, 4.0, 7.0, 12.0) if quick else (2.5, 4.0, 7.0, 12.0, 20.0)
+    for d, sphere_radius in ((4, 0.8), (7, 1.3), (11, 1.7)):
+        radial_weights = theta_weights * np.sin(theta) ** (d - 1)
+        radial_weights /= np.sum(radial_weights)
+        angular_weights = phi_weights * np.sin(phi) ** (d - 2)
+        angular_weights /= np.sum(angular_weights)
+        cosines = np.cos(phi)
+        exponent = (d + 1) / 2
+        variance = sphere_radius**2 / d
+        radius_squared = radius_nodes**2
+        b_sigma = exponent * (
+            -sphere_radius**2 / (1 + radius_squared)
+            + (d + 3)
+            * variance
+            * radius_squared
+            / (1 + radius_squared) ** 2
+        )
+        q_sigma = 0.5 * float(np.sum(radial_weights * b_sigma**2))
+
+        ratios = []
+        identity_errors = []
+        minimum_defect = math.inf
+        rejected_times = []
+        valid_times = []
+        for time in times:
+            epsilon = sphere_radius / time
+            denominator = (
+                1
+                + radius_squared[:, None]
+                + epsilon**2
+                - 2 * radius_nodes[:, None] * epsilon * cosines[None, :]
+            )
+            quotient_values = np.sum(
+                angular_weights[None, :]
+                * ((1 + radius_squared[:, None]) / denominator) ** exponent,
+                axis=1,
+            )
+            proxy_values = 1 + b_sigma / time**2
+            if np.min(proxy_values) <= 0:
+                rejected_times.append(time)
+                continue
+            valid_times.append(time)
+            entropy = float(
+                np.sum(
+                    radial_weights
+                    * quotient_values
+                    * np.log(quotient_values)
+                )
+            )
+            defect = float(
+                np.sum(
+                    radial_weights
+                    * quotient_values
+                    * np.log(quotient_values / proxy_values)
+                )
+            )
+            cross_entropy = float(
+                np.sum(
+                    radial_weights
+                    * quotient_values
+                    * np.log(proxy_values)
+                )
+            )
+            identity_errors.append(abs(entropy - defect - cross_entropy))
+            minimum_defect = min(minimum_defect, defect)
+            ratios.append(time**4 * cross_entropy / q_sigma)
+
+        row_ok = (
+            max(identity_errors) < 2.0e-13
+            and minimum_defect > -2.0e-13
+            and abs(ratios[-1] - 1) < (0.012 if quick else 0.005)
+            and abs(ratios[-1] - 1) < abs(ratios[0] - 1)
+        )
+        passed = passed and row_ok
+        rows.append(
+            f"d={d}, sphere radius={sphere_radius}: "
+            f"valid t={valid_times}, positivity-gate rejections={rejected_times}, "
+            f"t^4 cross/Q ratios={ratios}, "
+            f"max chain residual={max(identity_errors):.3e}, "
+            f"min proxy KL={minimum_defect:.3e}"
+        )
+    return Check(
+        "covariance proxy multidimensional quadrature",
+        passed,
+        "; ".join(rows),
+    )
+

+def finite_covariance_proxy_check(quick: bool) -> Check:
+    """Stress the proxy cross-entropy asymptotic on anisotropic atomic laws."""
+
+    rows = []
+    passed = True
+    sample_power = 18
+    times = (4.0, 7.0, 12.0) if quick else (4.0, 7.0, 12.0, 20.0)
+
+    for d in (4, 7):
+        sobol = qmc.Sobol(d=d + 1, scramble=True, seed=1729 + d)
+        uniform = sobol.random_base2(sample_power)
+        normal = ndtri(np.clip(uniform, 1.0e-14, 1.0 - 1.0e-14))
+        y = normal[:, :d] / np.abs(normal[:, d, None])
+        y_norm_sq = np.sum(y * y, axis=1)
+
+        pair_masses = np.linspace(0.45 / d, 0.75 / d, d)
+        pair_masses *= 0.72 / np.sum(pair_masses)
+        radii = np.linspace(0.65, 2.15, d)
+        covariance = np.diag(pair_masses * radii**2)
+        trace = float(np.trace(covariance))
+        covariance_zero = covariance - trace * np.eye(d) / d
+        iso = 3 * (d + 1) * (7 * d + 9) / (
+            4 * d * (d + 3) * (d + 5) * (d + 7)
+        )
+        traceless = 3 * (d + 1) * (d + 3) / (4 * (d + 5) * (d + 7))
+        q_value = iso * trace**2 + traceless * float(
+            np.sum(covariance_zero * covariance_zero)
+        )
+
+        quadratic = np.einsum("ni,ij,nj->n", y, covariance, y)
+        b_mode = (d + 1) / 2 * (
+            -trace / (1 + y_norm_sq)
+            + (d + 3) * quadratic / (1 + y_norm_sq) ** 2
+        )
+
+        cross_ratios = []
+        scaled_defects = []
+        for time in times:
+            quotient = np.full(y.shape[0], 1.0 - np.sum(pair_masses))
+            for axis, (mass, radius) in enumerate(zip(pair_masses, radii)):
+                shift = radius / time
+                dot = y[:, axis]
+                plus_denominator = 1 + y_norm_sq + shift**2 - 2 * shift * dot
+                minus_denominator = 1 + y_norm_sq + shift**2 + 2 * shift * dot
+                quotient += 0.5 * mass * (
+                    ((1 + y_norm_sq) / plus_denominator) ** ((d + 1) / 2)
+                    + ((1 + y_norm_sq) / minus_denominator) ** ((d + 1) / 2)
+                )
+
+            delta = quotient - 1
+            u = b_mode / time**2
+            remainder = delta - u
+            proxy_entropy = np.mean(
+                (1 + u) * np.log1p(u) - u
+                + remainder * np.log1p(u)
+            )
+            proxy_divergence = np.mean(
+                (1 + delta) * np.log((1 + delta) / (1 + u))
+                - remainder
+            )
+            cross_ratios.append(time**4 * proxy_entropy / q_value)
+            scaled_defects.append(time**4 * proxy_divergence)
+
+        row_ok = (
+            all(np.isfinite(cross_ratios))
+            and all(np.isfinite(scaled_defects))
+            and all(value >= -2.0e-10 for value in scaled_defects)
+            and abs(cross_ratios[-1] - 1.0) < (0.03 if quick else 0.012)
+            and abs(cross_ratios[-1] - 1.0) < abs(cross_ratios[0] - 1.0)
+            and scaled_defects[-1] < scaled_defects[0]
+        )
+        passed = passed and row_ok
+        rows.append(
+            f"d={d}: t^4 cross/Q={cross_ratios}, "
+            f"t^4 D(h||k)={scaled_defects}"
+        )
+
+    return Check(
+        "finite-covariance proxy asymptotic stress",
+        passed,
+        "; ".join(rows),
+    )
+
+

     """Stress the uniform Phi-Bregman bound at zero density and large spikes."""

+def covariance_proxy_quadrature_check(quick: bool) -> Check:
+    """Test the proxy chain identity and cross-entropy coefficient in d>=4."""
+
+    node_count = 70 if quick else 130
+    nodes, weights = leggauss(node_count)
+    theta = (math.pi / 4) * (nodes + 1)
+    theta_weights = (math.pi / 4) * weights
+    phi = (math.pi / 2) * (nodes + 1)
+    phi_weights = (math.pi / 2) * weights
+    radius_nodes = np.tan(theta)
+
+    rows = []
+    passed = True
+    times = (2.5, 4.0, 7.0, 12.0) if quick else (2.5, 4.0, 7.0, 12.0, 20.0)
+    for d, sphere_radius in ((4, 0.8), (7, 1.3), (11, 1.7)):
+        radial_weights = theta_weights * np.sin(theta) ** (d - 1)
+        radial_weights /= np.sum(radial_weights)
+        angular_weights = phi_weights * np.sin(phi) ** (d - 2)
+        angular_weights /= np.sum(angular_weights)
+        cosines = np.cos(phi)
+        exponent = (d + 1) / 2
+        variance = sphere_radius**2 / d
+        radius_squared = radius_nodes**2
+        b_sigma = exponent * (
+            -sphere_radius**2 / (1 + radius_squared)
+            + (d + 3)
+            * variance
+            * radius_squared
+            / (1 + radius_squared) ** 2
+        )
+        q_sigma = 0.5 * float(np.sum(radial_weights * b_sigma**2))
+
+        ratios = []
+        identity_errors = []
+        minimum_defect = math.inf
+        rejected_times = []
+        valid_times = []
+        for time in times:
+            epsilon = sphere_radius / time
+            denominator = (
+                1
+                + radius_squared[:, None]
+                + epsilon**2
+                - 2 * radius_nodes[:, None] * epsilon * cosines[None, :]
+            )
+            quotient_values = np.sum(
+                angular_weights[None, :]
+                * ((1 + radius_squared[:, None]) / denominator) ** exponent,
+                axis=1,
+            )
+            proxy_values = 1 + b_sigma / time**2
+            if np.min(proxy_values) <= 0:
+                rejected_times.append(time)
+                continue
+            valid_times.append(time)
+            entropy = float(
+                np.sum(
+                    radial_weights
+                    * quotient_values
+                    * np.log(quotient_values)
+                )
+            )
+            defect = float(
+                np.sum(
+                    radial_weights
+                    * quotient_values
+                    * np.log(quotient_values / proxy_values)
+                )
+            )
+            cross_entropy = float(
+                np.sum(
+                    radial_weights
+                    * quotient_values
+                    * np.log(proxy_values)
+                )
+            )
+            identity_errors.append(abs(entropy - defect - cross_entropy))
+            minimum_defect = min(minimum_defect, defect)
+            ratios.append(time**4 * cross_entropy / q_sigma)
+
+        row_ok = (
+            max(identity_errors) < 2.0e-13
+            and minimum_defect > -2.0e-13
+            and abs(ratios[-1] - 1) < (0.012 if quick else 0.005)
+            and abs(ratios[-1] - 1) < abs(ratios[0] - 1)
+        )
+        passed = passed and row_ok
+        rows.append(
+            f"d={d}, sphere radius={sphere_radius}: "
+            f"valid t={valid_times}, positivity-gate rejections={rejected_times}, "
+            f"t^4 cross/Q ratios={ratios}, "
+            f"max chain residual={max(identity_errors):.3e}, "
+            f"min proxy KL={minimum_defect:.3e}"
+        )
+    return Check(
+        "covariance proxy multidimensional quadrature",
+        passed,
+        "; ".join(rows),
+    )
+
+
 def quotient(y: mp.mpf, t: mp.mpf, law: Sequence[tuple[mp.mpf, mp.mpf]]) -> mp.mpf:
     return sum(weight * (1 + y * y) / (1 + (y - location / t) ** 2) for weight, location in law)

+            "; ".join(proxy_rows),
+        )
+    )
+
+    delta_symbol, u_symbol, log_delta, log_u = sp.symbols(
+        "delta u log_delta log_u"
+    )
+    chain_residual = sp.expand(
+        (1 + delta_symbol) * log_delta
+        - (1 + delta_symbol) * (log_delta - log_u)
+        - (1 + delta_symbol) * log_u
+    )
+    checks.append(
+        Check(
+            "covariance proxy exact KL chain identity",
+            chain_residual == 0,
+            f"pointwise residual={chain_residual}",
+        )
+    )
     return checks
 
 
+def finite_covariance_proxy_check(quick: bool) -> Check:
+    """Stress the proxy cross-entropy asymptotic on anisotropic atomic laws."""
+
+    rows = []
+    passed = True
+    sample_power = 18
+    times = (4.0, 7.0, 12.0) if quick else (4.0, 7.0, 12.0, 20.0)
+
+    for d in (4, 7):
+        sobol = qmc.Sobol(d=d + 1, scramble=True, seed=1729 + d)
+        uniform = sobol.random_base2(sample_power)
+        normal = ndtri(np.clip(uniform, 1.0e-14, 1.0 - 1.0e-14))
+        y = normal[:, :d] / np.abs(normal[:, d, None])
+        y_norm_sq = np.sum(y * y, axis=1)
+
+        pair_masses = np.linspace(0.45 / d, 0.75 / d, d)
+        pair_masses *= 0.72 / np.sum(pair_masses)
+        radii = np.linspace(0.65, 2.15, d)
+        covariance = np.diag(pair_masses * radii**2)
+        trace = float(np.trace(covariance))
+        covariance_zero = covariance - trace * np.eye(d) / d
+        iso = 3 * (d + 1) * (7 * d + 9) / (
+            4 * d * (d + 3) * (d + 5) * (d + 7)
+        )
+        traceless = 3 * (d + 1) * (d + 3) / (4 * (d + 5) * (d + 7))
+        q_value = iso * trace**2 + traceless * float(
+            np.sum(covariance_zero * covariance_zero)
+        )
+
+        quadratic = np.einsum("ni,ij,nj->n", y, covariance, y)
+        b_mode = (d + 1) / 2 * (
+            -trace / (1 + y_norm_sq)
+            + (d + 3) * quadratic / (1 + y_norm_sq) ** 2
+        )
+
+        cross_ratios = []
+        scaled_defects = []
+        for time in times:
+            quotient = np.full(y.shape[0], 1.0 - np.sum(pair_masses))
+            for axis, (mass, radius) in enumerate(zip(pair_masses, radii)):
+                shift = radius / time
+                dot = y[:, axis]
+                plus_denominator = 1 + y_norm_sq + shift**2 - 2 * shift * dot
+                minus_denominator = 1 + y_norm_sq + shift**2 + 2 * shift * dot
+                quotient += 0.5 * mass * (
+                    ((1 + y_norm_sq) / plus_denominator) ** ((d + 1) / 2)
+                    + ((1 + y_norm_sq) / minus_denominator) ** ((d + 1) / 2)
+                )
+
+            delta = quotient - 1
+            u = b_mode / time**2
+            remainder = delta - u
+            proxy_entropy = np.mean(
+                (1 + u) * np.log1p(u) - u
+                + remainder * np.log1p(u)
+            )
+            proxy_divergence = np.mean(
+                (1 + delta) * np.log((1 + delta) / (1 + u))
+                - remainder
+            )
+            cross_ratios.append(time**4 * proxy_entropy / q_value)
+            scaled_defects.append(time**4 * proxy_divergence)
+
+        row_ok = (
+            all(np.isfinite(cross_ratios))
+            and all(np.isfinite(scaled_defects))
+            and all(value >= -2.0e-10 for value in scaled_defects)
+            and abs(cross_ratios[-1] - 1.0) < (0.03 if quick else 0.012)
+            and abs(cross_ratios[-1] - 1.0) < abs(cross_ratios[0] - 1.0)
+            and scaled_defects[-1] < scaled_defects[0]
+        )
+        passed = passed and row_ok
+        rows.append(
+            f"d={d}: t^4 cross/Q={cross_ratios}, "
+            f"t^4 D(h||k)={scaled_defects}"
+        )
+
+    return Check(
+        "finite-covariance proxy asymptotic stress",
+        passed,
+        "; ".join(rows),
+    )
+
+

     """Stress the uniform Phi-Bregman bound at zero density and large spikes."""

+def covariance_proxy_quadrature_check(quick: bool) -> Check:
+    """Test the proxy chain identity and cross-entropy coefficient in d>=4."""
+
+    node_count = 70 if quick else 130
+    nodes, weights = leggauss(node_count)
+    theta = (math.pi / 4) * (nodes + 1)
+    theta_weights = (math.pi / 4) * weights
+    phi = (math.pi / 2) * (nodes + 1)
+    phi_weights = (math.pi / 2) * weights
+    radius_nodes = np.tan(theta)
+
+    rows = []
+    passed = True
+    times = (2.5, 4.0, 7.0, 12.0) if quick else (2.5, 4.0, 7.0, 12.0, 20.0)
+    for d, sphere_radius in ((4, 0.8), (7, 1.3), (11, 1.7)):
+        radial_weights = theta_weights * np.sin(theta) ** (d - 1)
+        radial_weights /= np.sum(radial_weights)
+        angular_weights = phi_weights * np.sin(phi) ** (d - 2)
+        angular_weights /= np.sum(angular_weights)
+        cosines = np.cos(phi)
+        exponent = (d + 1) / 2
+        variance = sphere_radius**2 / d
+        radius_squared = radius_nodes**2
+        b_sigma = exponent * (
+            -sphere_radius**2 / (1 + radius_squared)
+            + (d + 3)
+            * variance
+            * radius_squared
+            / (1 + radius_squared) ** 2
+        )
+        q_sigma = 0.5 * float(np.sum(radial_weights * b_sigma**2))
+
+        ratios = []
+        identity_errors = []
+        minimum_defect = math.inf
+        rejected_times = []
+        valid_times = []
+        for time in times:
+            epsilon = sphere_radius / time
+            denominator = (
+                1
+                + radius_squared[:, None]
+                + epsilon**2
+                - 2 * radius_nodes[:, None] * epsilon * cosines[None, :]
+            )
+            quotient_values = np.sum(
+                angular_weights[None, :]
+                * ((1 + radius_squared[:, None]) / denominator) ** exponent,
+                axis=1,
+            )
+            proxy_values = 1 + b_sigma / time**2
+            if np.min(proxy_values) <= 0:
+                rejected_times.append(time)
+                continue
+            valid_times.append(time)
+            entropy = float(
+                np.sum(
+                    radial_weights
+                    * quotient_values
+                    * np.log(quotient_values)
+                )
+            )
+            defect = float(
+                np.sum(
+                    radial_weights
+                    * quotient_values
+                    * np.log(quotient_values / proxy_values)
+                )
+            )
+            cross_entropy = float(
+                np.sum(
+                    radial_weights
+                    * quotient_values
+                    * np.log(proxy_values)
+                )
+            )
+            identity_errors.append(abs(entropy - defect - cross_entropy))
+            minimum_defect = min(minimum_defect, defect)
+            ratios.append(time**4 * cross_entropy / q_sigma)
+
+        row_ok = (
+            max(identity_errors) < 2.0e-13
+            and minimum_defect > -2.0e-13
+            and abs(ratios[-1] - 1) < (0.012 if quick else 0.005)
+            and abs(ratios[-1] - 1) < abs(ratios[0] - 1)
+        )
+        passed = passed and row_ok
+        rows.append(
+            f"d={d}, sphere radius={sphere_radius}: "
+            f"valid t={valid_times}, positivity-gate rejections={rejected_times}, "
+            f"t^4 cross/Q ratios={ratios}, "
+            f"max chain residual={max(identity_errors):.3e}, "
+            f"min proxy KL={minimum_defect:.3e}"
+        )
+    return Check(
+        "covariance proxy multidimensional quadrature",
+        passed,
+        "; ".join(rows),
+    )
+
+
 def quotient(y: mp.mpf, t: mp.mpf, law: Sequence[tuple[mp.mpf, mp.mpf]]) -> mp.mpf:
     return sum(weight * (1 + y * y) / (1 + (y - location / t) ** 2) for weight, location in law)

+def finite_covariance_proxy_check(quick: bool) -> Check:
+    """Stress the proxy cross-entropy asymptotic on anisotropic atomic laws."""
+
+    rows = []
+    passed = True
+    sample_power = 18
+    times = (4.0, 7.0, 12.0) if quick else (4.0, 7.0, 12.0, 20.0)
+
+    for d in (4, 7):
+        sobol = qmc.Sobol(d=d + 1, scramble=True, seed=1729 + d)
+        uniform = sobol.random_base2(sample_power)
+        normal = ndtri(np.clip(uniform, 1.0e-14, 1.0 - 1.0e-14))
+        y = normal[:, :d] / np.abs(normal[:, d, None])
+        y_norm_sq = np.sum(y * y, axis=1)
+
+        pair_masses = np.linspace(0.45 / d, 0.75 / d, d)
+        pair_masses *= 0.72 / np.sum(pair_masses)
+        radii = np.linspace(0.65, 2.15, d)
+        covariance = np.diag(pair_masses * radii**2)
+        trace = float(np.trace(covariance))
+        covariance_zero = covariance - trace * np.eye(d) / d
+        iso = 3 * (d + 1) * (7 * d + 9) / (
+            4 * d * (d + 3) * (d + 5) * (d + 7)
+        )
+        traceless = 3 * (d + 1) * (d + 3) / (4 * (d + 5) * (d + 7))
+        q_value = iso * trace**2 + traceless * float(
+            np.sum(covariance_zero * covariance_zero)
+        )
+
+        quadratic = np.einsum("ni,ij,nj->n", y, covariance, y)
+        b_mode = (d + 1) / 2 * (
+            -trace / (1 + y_norm_sq)
+            + (d + 3) * quadratic / (1 + y_norm_sq) ** 2
+        )
+
+        cross_ratios = []
+        scaled_defects = []
+        for time in times:
+            quotient = np.full(y.shape[0], 1.0 - np.sum(pair_masses))
+            for axis, (mass, radius) in enumerate(zip(pair_masses, radii)):
+                shift = radius / time
+                dot = y[:, axis]
+                plus_denominator = 1 + y_norm_sq + shift**2 - 2 * shift * dot
+                minus_denominator = 1 + y_norm_sq + shift**2 + 2 * shift * dot
+                quotient += 0.5 * mass * (
+                    ((1 + y_norm_sq) / plus_denominator) ** ((d + 1) / 2)
+                    + ((1 + y_norm_sq) / minus_denominator) ** ((d + 1) / 2)
+                )
+
+            delta = quotient - 1
+            u = b_mode / time**2
+            remainder = delta - u
+            proxy_entropy = np.mean(
+                (1 + u) * np.log1p(u) - u
+                + remainder * np.log1p(u)
+            )
+            proxy_divergence = np.mean(
+                (1 + delta) * np.log((1 + delta) / (1 + u))
+                - remainder
+            )
+            cross_ratios.append(time**4 * proxy_entropy / q_value)
+            scaled_defects.append(time**4 * proxy_divergence)
+
+        row_ok = (
+            all(np.isfinite(cross_ratios))
+            and all(np.isfinite(scaled_defects))
+            and all(value >= -2.0e-10 for value in scaled_defects)
+            and abs(cross_ratios[-1] - 1.0) < (0.03 if quick else 0.012)
+            and abs(cross_ratios[-1] - 1.0) < abs(cross_ratios[0] - 1.0)
+            and scaled_defects[-1] < scaled_defects[0]
+        )
+        passed = passed and row_ok
+        rows.append(
+            f"d={d}: t^4 cross/Q={cross_ratios}, "
+            f"t^4 D(h||k)={scaled_defects}"
+        )
+
+    return Check(
+        "finite-covariance proxy asymptotic stress",
+        passed,
+        "; ".join(rows),
+    )
+
+
